getPlayerField2             = 0x8006C994;

// Batch 67: 3x3 matrix inverse via adjugate/determinant (2026-05-11)
// Standard matrix inverse formula: M⁻¹ = adj(M) / det(M).
// Computes 3 cofactors (via cross-product pattern), then det = row0 · cofactor_col0.
// Divides 1.0/det and multiplies cofactors by it. Writes result into output rows at +0x0, +0xC, +0x18.
// 38 callers: a0=out 3x3 inverse, a1=in 3x3 matrix at offsets 0xC..0x20 (9 floats). Computes adjugate/determinant. Constant at 0x80000870 = 1.0.
mat3x3InverseInto = 0x8001C774;

// Batch 68: oriented NPC spawn helper (2026-05-11)
// Wraps spawnNpcOfType with an optional orientation. Builds a position+up-vector

// spawns the NPC with handler at 0x8006FC90.
// 54 callers: (position vec3, up vec3 or NULL, ...) — copies position to sp+0x10, up to sp+0x28 (default (0,1.0,0) if a2=NULL), calls orthonormalizeFrame, then spawnNpcOfType(0x8006FC90, sp+0x40, 1, 0xF, ...)
spawnOrientedNpc = 0x80072684;

// Batch 69: NPC slot chain destroyer (2026-05-11)

destroyNpcSlotChain = 0x8003F0C8;

// Batch 71 (2026-05-11): clearly-identifiable utility/getter/setter functions

// Asset-release primitive for the mesh-tree (HOB/HMT) family. Decrements
// refcount at *(u16*)(node+0x3A) of node = node[0]; if zero, walks 4

// Computes effective object detail level (range 0-3). Used to gate which LOD

// Object spawn/init — the canonical "create and initialize" function. Calls

// Quaternion multiply in (w, x, y, z) component order. Computes q1 * q2 → out:

// Batch 76 (2026-05-11): names derived from named-caller context. Each was

// Inner asset-lookup helper: when called with segment_id == -1, iterates 4
// segment-table entries at 0x80110A80 (each 0x50B) and calls find_manifest_entry
// per segment until a manifest entry matches. When called with a specific

// 21 callers, 10 of them named loaders — segment-fallback wrapper around find_manifest_entry

// (segment_idx | -1=all) — walks gManifestTable (4×0x50B entries) and, for each
// active segment (flag at +0x4E==1), iterates a 16-entry back-ref table at
// gManifestTable+0xBC0 (0x18B stride): zeroes entries whose +0x10 word matches
// the segment index, then rs_free's the segment data pointer at +0x40 and

// rs_malloc's 0x4380B scratch at 0x801394E8, fills indices 0..0x5F downward.
// resets NPC free-list + visibility buckets + per-frame scratch buffer
initNpcBookkeepingTables = 0x80056350;

// 43+ callers (all NPC update handlers): takes (npc_self, ?, ?, sortKey_float)

// load-balanced enqueue into 1 of 2 NPC render buckets

// Player-init common helper: called from every playerXxxInit (Awing, Falcon,

// craft's flags at gPlayers[craft_idx]+0x184, then branches on bit 0x40:
// when set (atmospheric/ground craft), stamps byte 1 at 0x8011A6B8 and uses
// the slot-chain at player+0x1CC; otherwise sets player+0x1C8=2, clears

// 10 callers, all playerXxxInit — sets up flight-vs-ground state from craft flags

// Per-player update-step helper: indexes per-player slot at 0x8010CF20 +

// func_80100594, then rs_free(slot->+0x8) and rs_free(slot->+0xC).

// Batch 77 (2026-05-11): asset-loader wrappers identified by the string
// literals they sprintf or pass to the loader. Each name encodes what the
// loaded asset IS — strongest possible semantic anchor.

// Level loader: sprintf "level/lv_%c" → calls loadDatFile, load_level_hmp,
// load_full_header_image. Composes the per-level asset basename from a
// character (e.g. 'a', 'b', 'c'...) and loads DAT/HMP/image for that level.
// 3 callers: (level_letter) → loads level DAT + HMP + image
loadLevelAssets = 0x800453C8;

// Level texture-cache loader: sprintf "ltex_cache_%d" + 'level/tex' literals,
// calls findAssetAcrossSegments + registerHmtTextureInTable. Loads cached
// level textures via HMT registry, registers them via the standard table.
// 3 callers: loads per-level texture cache (HMT-registered)
loadLevelTextureCache = 0x80048104;

// Sound/song asset loader: sprintf "sound/%s_SNG" → findAssetAcrossSegments
// + setupAssetDma. Top-level by-name SNG loader; 22 callers across the
// game's music/sfx subsystem.
// 22 callers: (song_name str) → loads "sound/<name>_SNG" via DMA
loadSongAssetByName = 0x8006657C;

// Font asset loader: sprintf "fonts/%s" → load_asset + registerHmtTextureInTable
// Loads a named font HMT and registers its textures.
// 4 callers: (font_name str) → load "fonts/<name>"
loadFontAsset = 0x80061A88;

// Player-craft shadow textures loader: loads "pl_crafts/shadows_TM" via
// load_asset, registers via registerHmtTextureInTable. The shared shadow
// texture set used by all flyable craft.
// 4 callers: loads pl_crafts/shadows_TM
loadCraftShadowTextures  = 0x8006B710;
// gates on gGameSettings+0xB63 (hi-res mode flag); loads explh_tex/explo2hi_IMGS (hi-res) or explo_tex/explo2_IMGS (lo-res)
loadExplosionTextures    = 0x8006108C;
// (parent_struct) — rs_malloc(0x2C90) + func_80054EF0("gnrc_stuff/tl_clouds"); stores buffer at parent[+0xF00], inits ~16 sub-struct slots
loadTopCloudsAsset       = 0x8005A120;
// NPC update handler for "debris1" type (per string scan); state machine dispatched via a1 in 0..4+
npcDebris1Update         = 0x8006F6A0;
// (npc_ctx, ...) → calls spawnNpcOfType(npcDebris1Update=0x8006F6A0, sp+0x10, 1, 0xF, ...); 26 callers (debris spawn helper)
spawnDebris1Npc          = 0x80072384;
// thin wrapper: getPlayerVehicleId(0) — convenience getter for player 0's vehicle ID
getLocalPlayerVehicleId  = 0x800C7524;
// per-frame HUD struct updater (per docs/unsorted_thoughts: "very involved in [hud_struct] innerworkings"); called from 10+ playerXxxUpdate functions
updatePlayerHudFrame     = 0x800FD31C;
// (texLutIdx u16) — clears D_8011A444[idx]; scans for other refs to same D_80128F08_index; if none, frees texture data via func_8001E7D0 (refcount release)
releaseTextureLutEntry   = 0x80022790;
// (dlPtr, flags) — appends RDP state-setup commands to dlPtr based on flag bits: 0xE7000000 (PIPESYNC) header, then per-flag G_SETOTHERMODE_L/H variants, depth/alpha modes, scissor; used by mesh dispatch (per docs/unsorted_thoughts)
appendRdpStateDl         = 0x8000DE70;
// load_hmt_and_hob("gnrc_stuff/sfx") + init sub-structs at 0x8013A670; per string scan
loadSfxAsset             = 0x80071D20;
// NPC update handler for "wake" effect (per string scan); state machine on a1 in 0..4+
npcWakeUpdate            = 0x80076BE4;
// NPC update handler for "vortex" effect (per string scan)
npcVortexUpdate          = 0x80081ABC;
// NPC update handler for "shield" effect (5-case state machine on a1-1; per string scan); distinct from npcShieldGeneratorUpdate
npcShieldUpdate          = 0x8007AEF8;
// NPC update handler for "i_missile" (incoming missile) per string scan
npcIncomingMissileUpdate = 0x800ABD0C;
// NPC update handler for "wtrspray" effect (per string scan); per-frame counterpart to initWaterSprayParticles
npcWaterSprayUpdate      = 0x80072EC4;
// (pos, vel, ...) → calls spawnNpcOfType(npcWaterSprayUpdate=0x80072EC4, ...); per spawn-wrapper handler-addr analysis
spawnWaterSprayNpc       = 0x800777F0;
// (a0=stateA, a1=stateB) — if (a0==4&&a1==1) OR (a0==7&&a1==0): load_hmt_and_hob("frontend/cut_ywng/cut_ywng") + walkMeshdef0List("cut_ywng"), stores result at MEM_W(0x800B0B18); otherwise clears it
maybeLoadYwingCutscene   = 0x800AF0EC;

// Sky horizon HOB loader: getHobObjectByName("sky_horizon"), allocates and
// copies into a per-scene buffer. Initializes the skybox horizon mesh.
// 2 callers: loads "sky_horizon" HOB into scene buffer
loadSkyHorizonHob = 0x8005AB34;

// Voice subtitle system init: loads "voice_TXT" via loadVoiceTxtFile,
// allocates a 0x20-byte voice context at 0x80139010, initializes it (sets
// 0xFFFF sentinel at +0x0), and configures based on gBootConfig fields.
// 3 callers: bootstrap the voice-subtitle subsystem
initVoiceSubtitleSystem  = 0x80055A30;
// (textId u16, callback ptr) — writes to the 0x20-byte voice context at MEM[0x80139010]: stores textId at +0x0, calls getVoiceText(textId), stores callback at +0x8, zeros bytes at +0x2 and +0x3. Called from tickSpeechSubsystem when starting a new voice line.
setActiveVoiceLine       = 0x800562E0;
// sets halfword at +0x0 of the voice context at MEM[0x80139010] to 0xFFFF (sentinel "no active voice line"). Called from tickSpeechSubsystem when voice playback finishes.
clearActiveVoiceLine     = 0x80056330;
// (f12 delta_time) per-frame from cinematicLoopBody + runInMissionFrame: manages the voice subtitle line in the 0x20-byte context at MEM[0x80139010]. Checks gGameSettings bits to select fallback subtitle id (0x222 if +0x16 set, 0x223 if +0x10 bit 0x20 set), invalidates outdated lines, and ticks the active line's animation/lifetime.
tickVoiceSubtitleDisplay = 0x80055CB0;

// 76-case jump table dispatcher on NPC type id (0..0x4B). Returns the per-type
// asset name string (e.g. 'b_lrsp', 'megaturret', 'b_ttu_hi') or NULL for
// types without an associated asset. Pure lookup function (no allocations,
// no side effects).
// 2 callers: (npc_type_id u8) → const char* or NULL
getAssetNameForNpcType = 0x800AE98C;

// Backdrop asset loader: sprintf "backdrop/<name>" using format strings at
// 0x800A5B60. Mallocs a 0x38-byte context, memsets to 0, then load_asset's
// the named backdrop file. Caller-supplied name (a0) selects which backdrop.
// 5 callers: (name str, ...) → loads "backdrop/<name>"
loadBackdropAsset     = 0x800ADF74;
// per-frame hangar camera-orbit lerp (~98 float ops). Reads gHangarCurrentBayCraft → indexes gHangarBayDescriptorArrayPtr (stride 0x278), pulls target float from entry+0x8->+0x14, caches at gHangarCameraOrbitTargetCache. Accumulates gHangarCameraOrbitAccum += (delta / const_at_800A56DC) per frame for the continuous glide toward the current bay. Called from the hangar tick func_800AAD9C.
hangarTickCameraOrbit = 0x800ADB70;

// Scene init / per-level asset loader: zmemcpy's defaults (0x50 bytes from
// 0x80037560 = gBootConfig area), loads "game_TXT" via findManifestEntryByName,
// calls clearSceneBssRegion, allocNpcContextArrays, initNpcSlotList,
// loadSndFiles. The entry point for setting up a mission/scene's runtime state.
// 2 callers: bootstrap mission-scene runtime (text + NPCs + sound)
initSceneForMission = 0x800A9ABC;

// Particle pool allocator: mallocs 0x3600 bytes (128 entries of 0x6C each),
// stores at 0x80130BD0, links them into a per-index chain (-1 sentinel at
// idx=0, prev index at others). Also references 'part_pal' and 'part_spr'
// strings to load the particle palette and sprite atlas via load_asset.
// 4 callers: allocates particle pool + loads palette/sprite assets
allocAndInitParticlePool = 0x80040310;

// Weapons-HMT loader: load_hmt_and_hob("weapons"), then iterates 15 weapon
// names from the table at 0x8009ED00, calling getHobObjectByName for each,
// storing the resulting hob-object ptrs at 0x80139600+ and setting bit
// 0x10000 in each obj's flags chain.
// 3 callers: load "weapons" HMT, register 15 weapon HOBs by name
loadWeaponsHobObjects = 0x8005E7EC;

// Count trailing zero bits in a 16-bit value. When (a0 & 1) is set, returns 0.
// Otherwise shifts right until the low bit is set, counting iterations.
// Called from playerXxxInit to convert a craft-mask bit (e.g. 0x0008) to a
// craft index (e.g. 3). Behavior on a0==0 is unsafe (infinite loop in a path
// that assumes a high bit will eventually appear).
// 5 callers, all playerXxxInit — bit-to-index converter
countTrailingZeros16 = 0x800BF2F4;

// Math primitives. Two-instruction floating-point utilities that the compiler
// emitted as named functions rather than inlining (probably because of `-O2
// -mips2` ABI conventions for FPU call/return). Strong semantic names because
// the operation is the function — no game-domain context required.
// 5 callers: trunc.w.d + cvt.d.w — truncate-toward-zero on a double, returns as double
truncDouble  = 0x80093E54;
// ceil.w.d + cvt.d.w — ceiling of a double, returns as double
ceilDouble   = 0x80093E60;
// 5 callers: pure sqrt.s f0, f12 — single-precision square root
sqrtf_recomp = 0x80093E6C;

// SND-file dispatcher: 12 callers. Reads the SND-loader state globals at
// 0x8013BF50/0x8013BF54/0x8013BF4C plus 0x8009FCD0, packages them as args
// 1-3 for parseSndFiles, then forwards a0 (sub-file kind) and returns 1.
// 12 callers: (sub_file_kind) → parseSndFiles with prepared globals
parseLoadedSndSection = 0x80066918;

// osWritebackDCache wrapper: a0 = address, a1 = halfword count. Doubles a1 to
// get a byte count, then calls osWritebackDCache(a0, a1*2). 3 callers in the
// MusyX/SND path where buffers are sized in halfwords.
// 3 callers: (addr, halfword_count) → osWritebackDCache(addr, count * 2)
writebackDCacheHalfwords = 0x80092588;

// Per-player HUD-struct setup for the current secondary weapon. Indexes
// gHudStructArray (D_8010CA30) by player_idx, stamps secondaryWeapon/Count/
// Reset/IsAdvanced + crosshair flag from cheat-code state, builds 10 texture
// IDs via func_800554D8 in a loop, then copies hud_elements forward via a
// rotate-then-overwrite pattern. Documented in docs/hud_stuff/func_800FBE20.c.
// 3 callers: (player_idx, weapon, count, isAdvanced) — populates hud_struct slice
configurePlayerSecondaryWeaponHud = 0x800FBE20;
// state machine: switch on missionStats.secondaryWeapon (D_80130B10+0x2) ∈ {2..8} → check corresponding "advanced" bit (10/12/15) in gGameSettings.flags → call configurePlayerSecondaryWeaponHud(player_skip, secWeapon, secWeaponMax, is_advanced). Callers: playerAtStInit, playerAwingUpdate, playerFalconUpdate.
refreshPlayerSecondaryWeaponHud   = 0x800FEC98;

// Pause-screen init/update. On first call (when arg0->unk258==0 AND game
// state D_8010CA20==5), builds the dim-quad mini-hob: 4 vertices at
// ±0x100, single quad face, color (0,0,0,0). Plays UI sound effects via
// func_800880D8/func_8008E0B0. Documented in docs/hud_stuff/func_800C1D3C.c.
// 2 callers: (hud_ext_struct, transition_f32) — pause-screen dim quad setup + ongoing update
initOrUpdatePauseScreenDim = 0x800C1D3C;

// Pause-menu state machine. Dispatches on game state via 20-entry jump table
// at 0x800A66A8 covering pause-menu phases (entry anim, hover, exit anim,
// selection feedback). Operates on the hud_ext struct (func_800C0084_type).
// Documented in docs/hud_stuff/func_800C298C.c.
// 2 callers: (caller_ctx, hud_ext_struct) — 20-state pause menu dispatcher
runPauseMenuStateMachine = 0x800C298C;

// MusyX note-rate compute: a0=note_id, a1=context_word (-1 → default 0x40005622
// where the low 24 bits 0x5622=22050 is NTSC sample rate). Extracts the top
// byte of a1 as "base note", looks up a multiplier from one of two LUTs at
// 0x800A31D0 / 0x800A2FD0 based on sign of (note - base), multiplies by the
// low 24 bits of a1, takes high 32 bits >> 20, then forwards to func_80093F18.
// The classic semitone-relative pitch shift for sample playback.
// 10 callers: (note, ctx_or_-1) → adjusted playback rate
musyxComputeNotePlaybackRate = 0x80093E80;

// Returns the address of voiceTxtString (0x80138E90). Pure 2-instruction
// constant-pointer getter — used wherever code needs to write/read the voice
// text buffer's first slot.
// 2 callers: returns &voiceTxtString
getVoiceTxtStringPtr = 0x80055A20;

startSiPollingThread                    = 0x80002F4C;
// (req_ptr, count, ?) — adds `count` DMA requests to ring buffer at gDmaSlotRing (0x801112B0); checks queue free space (256 - (head-tail)+count+1 < 256) and dma mutex state != 2 (busy); called from mainBootstrapWorker boot-time asset batch
submitDmaRequestBatch                   = 0x8000569C;
// (dst=a0, src=mat3x4 ptr=a1) — converts a row-major 3x3 rotation + translation to N64 Mtx fixed-point format using packMtxRowFloatToFixed for each of 3 rotation rows; constants at 0x800186EC are scale factors
convertMat3x4ToN64Mtx                   = 0x80018D80;
// (a0-a3 = 4 signed shorts + ~9 word stack args + 3 hw stack args = 16 total) — DUAL ROLE: (1) writes GFX state globals at vram 0x80017C14-0x80017C38 — width to MEM_W[0x8011A838], 2nd word to MEM_W[0x8011A844], height halfword to MEM_H[0x8011A848], and current draw fb to MEM_W[0x8011A84C] (or 0 if flags bit 0x200 set); these are what the SET_COLOR_IMAGE emitter at 0x80009124 reads to fill DL bytes. (2) Copies 'mat_default' literal from strMatDefault (0x800007D0) onto local stack as material name, populates material entry from args, calls registerHmtTextureInTable (0x80022B90). Callers: advanceVideoFrame (per-frame) and initVideoBootWrapper (boot). The name is partly misleading — it's the gfx-state-and-default-material composite setter, not just material construction.
buildAndRegisterDefaultMaterial         = 0x80017B88;
// Called from teardown chain of all 10 player*Update funcs (after destroySceneNpcsAndUnlink + rs_free + releaseMeshAsset). (1) Releases 10 texture LUT entries via releaseTextureLutEntry from u16 array at 0x8010CA3A (matches notes.txt "8010CA3A is the start of a u16 array 10(?) elements big"). (2) Iterates 2 HUD-instance structs of 0x278 bytes starting at 0x8010CA30 (0x4F0 total = 2 * 0x278): for each, if byte+0x0==3||==8 AND byte+0x210!=0 → destroyNpcSlotChain(halfword@+0x212) to unbind bound NPCs; if byte+0x274!=0 → factor5RemoveListNode(instance+0x230) to detach from scene graph.
teardownHudInstancesAndUnbindNpcs       = 0x800FE434;
// 20 named callers — generic craft-NPC factory. Args: (f12=x, f14=y, a2=craftType_lowNibble). rs_malloc(0x1E4=484) for new NPC context, calls loadCraftShadowTextures(type), initializes ~15 fields of the NPC: +0x78=0x02960001 flags, +0x80=9 typeID, self-pointers at +0x30/+0x8C/+0x90 to inner offsets +0x6C/+0xC8/+0x178, clears +0x20..0x2C/+0x70..0x88, looks up craft-specific halfword from secondary table at 0x8013A528 (2*type stride), sets +0xD8 to constant data at 0x800A09C0, calls clearStructPair2C30(entry+0xC8). Returns the new NPC pointer in v0. Called from npc*Update funcs (AT-AT, AT-PT, AT-ST, GroundTransport, etc.) when a missile / debris / sub-craft needs to spawn — often from a 20-byte stride spawn-table at 0x800B04AC keyed by parent's slot index.
allocateAndInitCraftNpcContext          = 0x8006BD18;
// 17 named callers (across AT-AT, AT-PT, AT-ST, GroundTransport, ProbeDroid, PowerGenerator, RadarDish, etc. update funcs). Thin 2-step destroyer: (1) clearPendingChildNpc(MEM_W[a0+0]) clears pending-spawn state on the child reference; (2) func_800EC388(a0) unlinks the node from one of two doubly-linked lists at MEM[0x8011C0D0]/[0x8011C0D4] (chosen by node-byte0 zero check) when (node_byte0 + extra_byte_at_a0+4) == 4, then unconditionally rs_free()'s the node. Always returns 1.
clearPendingAndDestroyChildNpc          = 0x800EC684;
// 12 callers, ALL `lvN_initializeObjectTracking` (for every level 0..b). One-instruction setter: MEM_W[0x8013973C] = f12. Each level passes its own per-level float during init. The paired byte at MEM_B[0x8013973A] is cleared by other writers (func_8005E950) that set both fields. Read by an object-init function around 0x800DA478 which stores the value into a stack slot at sp+0xC4 — i.e., per-object setup absorbs the level-wide scalar. Purpose of the scalar (probably a distance/scale/threshold factor) unknown without deeper trace; the setter signature is unambiguous.
setLevelObjectTrackingScalar            = 0x800C7CEC;
// Sole non-internal caller is clearPendingAndDestroyChildNpc. Takes (a0=node-pair-struct). Reads node = MEM_W[a0+0] and extra_byte = MEM_BU[a0+4]. If (node_byte0 + extra_byte) == 4: unlinks the node from one of two doubly-linked lists by patching the prev/next pointers at node+0x8 / node+0xC, head ptr selected from MEM[0x8011C0D0] (when node_byte0 != 0) or MEM[0x8011C0D4] (when node_byte0 == 0). Then unconditionally rs_free(node). The (kind+extra)==4 condition is some refcount/state gate for "safe to detach".
unlinkAndFreeChildNpcNode               = 0x800EC388;
// 9 callers, ALL player*Update — initializes a craft animation slot. (a0=craft_ptr, a1=slotIdx&0xFF, a2=animTemplate_ptr). Indexes into 24-byte-stride table at 0x8010CF20 (= 0x8011 - 0x30E0): entry = base + 24 * slotIdx. Writes entry+0x0=a0 (craft), entry+0x4=a2 (template). Reads count from MEM_BU[a2+0]; if zero, skip alloc. Otherwise rs_malloc(count*2) twice and stores at entry+0x8 and entry+0xC — paired anim parameter buffers. Then runs an inner loop walking the template entries.
allocateCraftAnimationStateForSlot      = 0x801007C0;
// 9 callers, ALL player*Update — paired with allocateCraftAnimationStateForSlot. (a0=slotIdx&0xFF). Calls func_80100594(slotIdx) for pre-step, indexes same table at 0x8010CF20, clears 4 state bytes at entry+0x10..0x13, writes paired animation-timing globals: MEM_W[0x8010D028] = constant float from 0x800A9548, MEM_W[0x8010D02C] = MEM_W[craft+0xE8] (animation speed?). Effectively resets a craft's animation playback state.
resetCraftAnimationStateForSlot         = 0x80100FA8;
// 6 callers (controllerSettingsScreen, eliteRoguesMenuHandler, hangarInitialize, initVoiceSubtitleSystem, menuControllerInput, postLevelStatScreen) — 3-field setter on the menu/player-slot-table state header at 0x80139960: byte_state=a0, x_hw=a1, y_hw=a2. Same fields are reset by initAllPlayerSlots (state byte =1, x_hw=y_hw=0x140 default). Typical menu-screen call shape: setMenuOverlayHeaderState(1, 0, 0) at screen entry.
setMenuOverlayHeaderState               = 0x80063E2C;
// 5 callers in every frame-rendering top-level loop (cinematicLoopBody, menuControllerInput, menuSubtype02Handler, runIdleFramesAndLoadSaveData, runInMissionFrame). Begins a frame DL chunk via beginFrameDLChunk then emits 7 profiler bar segments via emitProfilerBarSegment (matches `project_builtin_profiler_overlay.md`: "Caller emits 7 bars/frame"). Each call pulls bar timing/color from global timing block at 0x8012DC50 / 0x8012A910 / 0x8012A914 with per-bar Y-offsets descending from screen-width-minus-0x28. Profiler is dormant in retail (gated by MEM_B[0x80038CE0] = 0) but codepath intact.
drawFrameProfilerBars                   = 0x8000B6F4;
// 6 callers (cutsceneDispatchSlotsByFlag, initCinematicDispatchSlots, loadPlayerStartPosition, npcShieldGeneratorUpdate, runInMissionFrame, runPauseMenuStateMachine). (a0=dst_9floats, a1=src). De-interleaves a 3x3 rotation/frame matrix from a scattered source layout (src offsets +0x24/+0x28/+0x2C, +0x08/+0x14/+0x20, +0x04/+0x10/+0x1C → dst+0x00..+0x20 packed), then calls orthonormalizeFrame(dst). Frame extraction for spline-following / cinematic-camera math — sibling to composeRotationMatrix*/mat34CrossRows/orthonormalizeFrame primitives.
extractOrthonormalFrameFromStruct       = 0x80059D18;
// 5 menu-lifecycle callers (gracefulMenuShutdown, menuControllerInput, menuOverlayInit, menuSubtype02Handler, waitForAnyAudioSlot). Gated on gMusyXActiveFlag (MEM_BU[0x800A0F50]); when active, takes factor5MutexAcquire, runs three MusyX sub-tasks: tickActiveSongs (iterates 8 song slots stride 0xEF8 base 0x80149A00 calling tickSongSequence per slot), func_80098EEC (unknown), func_8008DBF4(0) (unknown). Releases factor5Mutex on exit. Effectively the per-frame MusyX engine update from the menu/UI loop.
tickMusyXIfActive                       = 0x8008DA00;
// 4 callers (allocAndInitParticlePool, handleHUD, loadExplosionTextures, loadTopCloudsAsset). Asset DMA-and-process pipeline: (1) get_asset_size_extra(name) → size, (2) rs_malloc(size, align=4) with align=0 fallback, (3) get_asset_size_simple + findAssetAcrossSegments + setupAssetDma + teardownAssetDma to DMA the asset into the buffer, (4) iterate sub-chunks at the buffer head — each entry has a size field that's read and used as (size+7)&~3 stride to step through. Generic asset loader for assets with self-describing sub-chunk tables.
loadAssetAndIterateSubchunks            = 0x80054EF0;
// 4 callers in Actor type-1 family (executeActorType1Effect, initActorAsType1, initActorAsType1WithAnchor) + hangarInitialize. (a0, a1, a2, a3=actor_ptr, sp+0x38=fZ). Builds vec3 = (0, 0, fZ) on the stack (zero X/Y, caller-supplied forward), calls func_80065A60(...) which is the underlying 4-ptr+3-float transform helper to write into the actor's transform block, then clears actor+0x8 (the "base position ref" field per project_actor_effect_struct.md) so the actor uses local-frame instead of anchored ref. Per memory note, this is the specific anchor-transform setup that `initActorAsType1WithAnchor` uses to attach a type-1 actor's transform to its NPC anchor.
setupActorAnchorTransformType1          = 0x800662A0;
// 18 callers across npc*Update (Actor type-1/2/3/4 family). (a0=actor, a1=byte_flag, a2=out_render_descriptor, a3=mode_byte, sp+0x40=arg). Gated on MEM_BU[actor+0x1A7] — the "render descriptor initialized" flag per project_actor_effect_struct.md. When set, skips (descriptor already prepared this frame). When clear: marks out+0x6=0xFFFF (invalidate), early-exits if (a1&0xFF)!=0, checks 4-byte flags at actor+0x1A4 with 0xFF00FF00 mask (also early-exits if any selected bit set), then when a3==1 fetches NPC context via getNpcContextField8(actor+0x198) and copies its vec3 to out+0x10/+0x14/+0x18. First-time prep of the render descriptor; the init flag is set elsewhere in the caller chain after success.
initActorRenderDescriptorIfUninit       = 0x800F1E7C;
// 17 callers across Actor type-1/2/3/4 family npc*Update functions. (a0=actor, ...). Sets s0=actor+0x114, gates on motion-active flag MEM_BU[actor+0x135] (memory: +0x135 = motion-active byte), then byte at actor+0x134, then validates three anchor halfwords at actor+0x12A/+0x12C/+0x12E vs 0xFFFF sentinel. Calls findActiveNpcInSlotChain(slotHandle=halfword@+0x12C, 0) to resolve the NPC anchor, then performs LOS validation. Sister of func_800F09A8 ("computes delta vector after LOS" per memory). Per project_actor_effect_struct.md: this is the "target/LOS validation against NPC anchor".
validateActorTargetLOS                  = 0x800F07AC;
// 13 callers, sister of validateActorTargetLOS. Identical prologue: s0=actor+0x114, gates on motion-active flag at actor+0x135, gates on byte at actor+0x134, validates anchor halfword at actor+0x130 (NPC slot handle per project_actor_effect_struct.md) vs 0xFFFF sentinel. Then computes the delta vector from anchor NPC's world position to actor's local frame (per memory: "computes delta vector after LOS"). Used by the same Actor family npc*Update funcs after validateActorTargetLOS confirms anchor validity.
computeActorAnchorDeltaAfterLOS         = 0x800F09A8;
// 11 callers in Actor cluster — perpendicular emit-direction generator with random rotation. Gated on actor+0x162 (per project_actor_effect_struct.md: "duration/count for perpendicular emit"). Args: (a0=actor, a1=src_vecs_struct, a2=flag_byte). (1) vec3Cross(sp+0x10, src+0x18, src+0xC) — perpendicular axis from two source vectors. (2) Uses count at +0x162 as angle-bound; two rand_recomp calls produce angles in [-1, 1] / count via constants at 0x800A9018/0x800A901C. (3) quatFromAxisAngle(perp_axis, angle1) and quatFromAxisAngle(src+0x18, angle2). (4) quatMultiply → combined rotation. (5) rotateVec3ByQuat(src+0xC) → write back rotated vector. Result is a randomly-rotated direction around the perpendicular axis, used for spawning debris/particles with directional spread.
generatePerpEmitDirectionWithJitter     = 0x800F2DC8;
// 4 callers in Actor type-1 family. (a0=actor, a1=mat_target). Type-guard: actor+0x112 must == 1 (Actor type 1 per project_actor_effect_struct.md). Zero-gate: float at actor+0xF0 must == 0.0 (proceed only when this gate value is zero — likely "needs frame-sync this tick"). When both pass: getNpcContextField8(MEM_HU[actor+0x198]) → NPC anchor context, then mat34CrossRowsAndCopyPos(sp+0x10, ctx, 1.0) and matrix-inverse work to write the anchor's local frame into the actor's transform matrix. Per memory note: "type-1 large body, reads actor+0xF0 zero-gate then getNpcContextField8". Updates a type-1 actor's transform to track its NPC anchor when the zero-gate condition triggers.
syncType1ActorFrameToAnchorIfZeroGate   = 0x800F9C30;
// 15 callers in NPC update funcs (AT-AT, AT-PT, AT-ST, GroundTransport, PowerGenerator, ProbeDroid, RadarDish, ShieldGenerator, etc.). (a0=npc, ...). Reads PathState ptr at MEM[npc+0x8]; if NULL, exits with original position. Then gates on mission-mode at MEM_W[0x8010CA20]: when mode != 0 (paused/cinematic) raw-copies the PathState base position (v1+0x0/+0x4/+0x8) into the output stack vec3 (sp+0x10..+0x18). When mode == 0 (active gameplay) applies first-order Taylor back-extrapolation `out[i] = base[i] - vel[i] * f4` using scale constant at MEM_W[0x8003D1FC] — same Taylor pattern as named primitive vec3ExtrapolateFirstOrder (0x80077784, per project_spline_based_architecture.md), but reads the PathState from npc+0x8 rather than a caller-supplied struct. After the position fill, reads halfword type at npc+0x4 and continues with type-specific work for types in [8..14]. Predictive position used downstream for spatial effects (e.g. sound/shadow placement) that need "where will this NPC be in 1 frame".
extrapolateNpcPositionForCurrentMode    = 0x80076250;
// 13 callers — mix of npc*Update (AT-PT, GroundTransport, ProbeDroid, RadarDish, ShuttleUpdate) + npc*HandleDestruction (AT-AT, AT-ST, Shuttle). Args: (a0=position vec3 ptr, a2=f10 scaleA, a3=f14 scaleB, sp+0xB0=f6 scaleC). Matches the destruction-effect-burst signature from project_spline_based_architecture.md: spawnDebris1Npc + spawnOrientedNpc + spawnNpcOfType (×N) + composeRandomRotationMatrix + scaleVec3ByRandomRange + rand_recomp — produces the debris + oriented-debris + explosion-NPC bursts at the supplied world position with caller-tuned scale parameters. Shared spawn-cascade primitive called whenever an NPC takes critical damage or explodes, including from inside update funcs (likely "secondary explosion" mid-life cases) — not only from per-NPC destruction handlers.
spawnDestructionEffectBurst             = 0x80073750;
// Recursive binary-tree walker. (a0=anim_handle). Reads global ring head at MEM_W[0x8013AC10], advances head by 4 (consume one slot), writes (a0+0x1C) at the consumed slot — appends the handle's +0x1C value (anim-channel ref) to the flat output buffer. Then recurses on two children at a0+0xC and a0+0x0 when non-null. Effectively flattens an anim-handle tree into the global ring buffer at 0x8013AC10. Companion to flattenAnimTreeAndAllocateSlot which sets up the buffer + initial head before calling this.
appendAnimHandleAndDescendantsToBuffer  = 0x80082A98;
// 5 callers in AT-AT family (npcAtAtUpdate, npcAtPtUpdate, npcAtStUpdate, playerAtStUpdate, tickAtAtAudioAndWeaponState). (a0=anim_handle_root, a1=template_holder). Reads count from MEM_HU[MEM_W[a1+0x4]+0xE] (anim template entry count), rs_malloc(count*4) for a flat buffer, sets global ring head MEM_W[0x8013AC10] = buffer+4, writes (a0+0x1C) at buffer+0 (root). Calls appendAnimHandleAndDescendantsToBuffer on a0+0xC and a0+0x0 children to fill the buffer with the tree's flattened anim-channel refs. Finally calls allocateModelAnimSlot(a0, a1, count) and stamps secondary-active byte at slot[id-1]+0x11 (gActiveModelAnimSlots = 0x8013A990, 20-byte stride per project_actor_effect_struct.md). Returns the new slot id.
flattenAnimTreeAndAllocateSlot          = 0x80082B00;
// Per-slot keyframe vec3 sampler. (a0 = high 16 bits = 1-based slot index in gActiveModelAnimSlots, low 16 bits = sub-entry index; a1 = output vec3 ptr). Validates slot_idx in (0, 32] and slot[slot_idx-1]+0x10 (active byte) == 1. Reads slot+0x08 (entryCountSrc) then deref +0x4 for sub-entry table base, indexes by sub_idx*16 to get the 16-byte sub-entry. Checks bit 0x1 of MEM[sub_entry+0x4] — if clear, returns 0. Else reads 3 raw s16 halfwords at sub_entry+0x8/+0xA/+0xC, scales each by float at slot+0x0C (secondaryTable per project_actor_effect_struct.md) entry base (sub_idx*16 offset), then by constant float at 0x8003DB20 (likely fixed-point→float scale). Writes the resulting vec3 to a1+0x0/+0x4/+0x8. The "secondary-secondary-table" referenced in the actor-effect memory note is this 16-byte-stride sub-entry table reached through slot+0x08→deref+0x4.
sampleModelAnimKeyframeVec3             = 0x80083318;
// **THE canonical NPC slot handler** documented in project_npc_handler_protocol.md. Signature: (a0=slot, a1=action_code, a2=msg). Dispatches on a1: action 1 = spawn (popNpcSlotFromTail + recursive self-call to 0x1000 if msg non-null), action 2 = destroy, action 3 = tick, action 4 = active-check (returns v0=1), action 0x1000 = position-update (reads vec3 from msg+0x0/+0x4/+0x8, subtracts global scene origin at 0x801396D4, writes scaled into slot+0x10/+0x14/+0x18). Tick path (action 3) branches on bit 0x100 of slot+0xA0: when set, plays back a byte-encoded animation table at slot+0xA4 (4-byte-stride entries, b/u8/u8 = x/y/z per frame), advancing time accumulator at slot+0xB0 by (msg+0x0 * slot+0xAC speed), with slot+0xA8 = anim length and slot+0xA9 = max frames, wrapping or destroying on overrun (bit 0x200 of slot+0xA0 = "destroy on anim end"); when clear, walks child slot chain at slot+0x20. Used by 11+ wrappers (spawnByteAnimDebris*) in destruction handlers. The handler is the most common "lightweight pre-baked animation" NPC in the engine.
byteAnimatedDebrisNpcHandler            = 0x8004C6C8;
// 11 callers, mostly NPC destruction handlers + a few update funcs. Args: (a0=position_vec3_ptr). Builds a 16-byte spawn descriptor with pos.y-offset (subtracts constant float at MEM[0x8003B1A4]) and stores at sp+0x10..+0x18, fills additional fields (float at sp+0x1C from constant 0x8003B1A8, halfword 0x300 at sp+0x20, gActiveScene/effects ptr from MEM[0x80139740] at sp+0x24, byte 0 at sp+0x28, byte 0x10 at sp+0x29, float constant at sp+0x2C). Calls spawnNpcOfType(handler=byteAnimatedDebrisNpcHandler, descriptor=sp+0x10, type=2, idx=0xF). Thin wrapper that spawns the default-config byte-animated debris NPC at the supplied position with grounding Y-offset.
spawnByteAnimDebrisDefault              = 0x8005EBD8;
// Sister of spawnByteAnimDebrisDefault. Same handler (byteAnimatedDebrisNpcHandler) and same descriptor shape, but takes (a0=pos, a1=byte_param) — stores a1 at sp+0x28 and a1+0x10 at sp+0x29 (vs both bytes hardcoded 0/0x10 in the default variant). Uses a different set of float scale constants from 0x8003B1B0/0x8003B1B4/0x8003B1B8 area, suggesting a different debris visual variant tuned by the caller-supplied byte param.
spawnByteAnimDebrisWithParam            = 0x8005EC60;
// NPC slot handler following project_npc_handler_protocol.md (a0=slot, a1=action, a2=msg). Action 1 (spawn) path: reads craft type from MEM_HU[msg+0xC], pos from MEM_W[msg+0x4]/+0x8, calls allocateAndInitCraftNpcContext(x, y, type) → new NPC ctx, stores at slot+0xC, initializes the path state (clears NPC+0x10, sets NPC+0x14 to constant 0x8003C9A0), then calls advanceNpcOnCurvedPath(...) to seed the spline trajectory and finally popNpcSlotFromTail to claim the slot. Copies NPC's path-anchor vec3 (NPC+0x60/+0x64/+0x68) into slot+0x0/+0x4/+0x8. This is THE craft-on-spline spawner — used by every enemy craft that flies a predefined curved path (per project_spline_based_architecture.md).
splineWalkerCraftNpcHandler             = 0x8006C3A0;
// 7 callers across AT-AT/AT-PT/AT-ST destruction handlers + GroundTransport/GroundTroop/RadarDish/TankDroid update funcs. Thin wrapper around spawnNpcOfType: builds 16-byte descriptor on stack (a0/a1/a2/a3=pos+type fields, a3=hw_param at sp+0x1C), then calls spawnNpcOfType(handler=splineWalkerCraftNpcHandler, descriptor=sp+0x10, type=1, subtype=0xA). Spawns a craft NPC that will fly a curved path — typical use: "after this large unit's death, spawn an escape craft / replacement squadron".
spawnSplineWalkerCraft                  = 0x8006C6FC;
// NPC slot handler per project_npc_handler_protocol.md. 10 spawn-sites from playerSnowSpeederUpdate (snow speeder's audio attachments). Action 1: reads parent_slot_idx halfword from msg+0x0 → stores at slot+0x0, reads sound_template_idx halfword from msg+0x2 → stores at slot+0x4, computes 24-byte-stride table index at MEM[0x8010BB00 + template_idx*24] (= 0x8011 - 0x6500 base), stores template ptr at slot+0x4C. Calls getNpcContextField8 to fetch parent NPC ctx; if null, self-destructs. Else: validates parent gear/state, calls play3DSoundEvent with templated 3-byte audio params (vol/pan/pitch) from the template entry and parent NPC's audio anchor at parentCtx+0x24. Action 2: destroyNpcSlotChain. Action 3 (tick): re-emit / position-track sound to follow parent. The 24-byte template table at 0x8010BB00 holds per-effect-type 3D sound presets — the snow speeder spawns 10 distinct sound instances (engine, cockpit, lasers, tow cable, etc.) via this handler.
attached3DSoundEmitterNpcHandler        = 0x800BF4A0;
// NPC slot handler per project_npc_handler_protocol.md. 9 spawn-sites from playerSnowSpeederUpdate (snow speeder's mesh attachments). Action 1: reads parent_slot_idx from msg+0x0, fetches parent context via getNpcContextByIndex; if parent_ctx+0xC (mesh part ref) is NULL, self-destructs. Otherwise calls dispatchOverlaySetupByParentAssetName(parentMeshPart, slot, parent_slot_idx) to per-asset-name route the overlay setup, then writes constant data pointer D_8009EBD0 (mesh template?) into parentMeshPart+0x4 — replacing the parent's mesh component with this attachment's geometry. Action 2 (destroy): iterates 2 buffers at slot+0x1C/+0x20 and rs_free's each (geometry buffers allocated during overlay setup). Used to attach detachable cosmetic / visual components to a parent NPC (tow cable rigging, weapon mounts, exhaust trails, etc.).
attachedMeshOverlayNpcHandler           = 0x8005427C;
// Inner helper for attachedMeshOverlayNpcHandler (Action 1). (a0=overlay_slot, a1=parent_slot_id_hw). Reads parent NPC's HMT asset name string at MEM[overlay_slot+0x0+0x0], then does cascading rs_strcmp comparisons against a series of asset-name constants at 0x8003ABFC/0x8003AC04/0x8003AC0C/etc. — per-craft routing of the overlay setup (different code path for snow speeder vs other parent vehicles). The string-keyed dispatch is how the snow speeder's 9 distinct mesh attachments get configured: same handler, parent-asset-name selects which sub-handler logic runs.
dispatchOverlaySetupByParentAssetName   = 0x80053C04;
// **THE oriented-NPC handler** used by spawnOrientedNpc (54 callers via that wrapper, plus 4 direct spawn-sites). Per project_npc_handler_protocol.md signature. Action 1 (spawn): copies 4 words (a position-and-orientation matrix block) from msg+0x0..+0xC to slot's mesh-state at s2+0x4. Action 2 (destroy): cleanup path. Action 3 (tick) and Action 4 (active-check) handled. The defining feature is that this handler expects the caller to supply BOTH a position AND a pre-orthonormalized 3x3 frame in the spawn descriptor — used for debris/effects that need custom orientation at spawn time (e.g. impact sparks aligned with surface normals, oriented engine trails, surface-decals). Per project_recomp_function_map.md and existing spawnOrientedNpc docs, this is the most commonly-used NPC handler in the engine (54+ usages just through spawnOrientedNpc).
orientedNpcHandler                      = 0x8006FC90;
// NPC slot handler per project_npc_handler_protocol.md. 3 spawn-sites from func_80074600 (an explosion-trigger function). Action 1 (spawn): stamps type id 0x201 at slot+0x40, copies position vec3 from msg+0x0/+0x4/+0x8, copies asset name strings (unaligned 4-byte words) at msg+0x4→slot+0x5C and msg+0x8→slot+0x60, stores velocity floats at slot+0x4C/+0x50/+0x54, calls composeRandomRotationMatrix(slot+0x18, ..., 0x32) for tumbling orientation, then orthonormalizeFrame on slot+0xC. Crucially, the Action 1 path then internally calls spawnDebris1Npc + spawnOrientedNpc + spawnNpcOfType (multiple) + rand_recomp + play3DSoundEvent — the full destruction-effect-burst signature from project_spline_based_architecture.md. So this handler IS a self-sustaining "explosion controller" NPC: a single spawn creates the controller plus all its debris/spray/oriented-decal children, and the controller persists to manage the explosion lifecycle.
explosionEffectNpcHandler               = 0x8007413C;
// NPC slot handler per project_npc_handler_protocol.md. 2 spawn-sites, primary caller setupCutsceneLevel. Action 1 (spawn): rs_malloc(0x118=280 bytes) for a cinematic-actor context, stores at slot+0x4. Initializes TWO parallel sub-blocks in the 280-byte ctx: (1) first block at ctx+0x4C..+0xB0 with callback fn ptr at ctx+0x58 (a 0x80040000-range function), inner-pointer at ctx+0xAC = ctx+0x4C (self-ref), zeroes accumulators at +0x5C/+0x60/+0x64/+0x6C/+0x70/+0x74, calls clearStructPair2C30 (clears a paired sub-struct). (2) second block at ctx+0xB4..+0xD0 with callback fn ptr at ctx+0xB4 (a 0x80050000-range function), zeroes accumulators at +0xB8/+0xBC/+0xC0/+0xC8/+0xCC/+0xD0, calls clearStructPair2C30 again. Also clears ctx+0x0..+0x14 (early vec4-like fields). Calls func_8006B3F0 + func_8006B458 (other init helpers). The dual-callback structure suggests a scripted cinematic actor with separate transform-channel and behavior-channel state machines — likely the per-actor cinematic playback NPC instantiated for each Cutscene actor.
cutsceneActorNpcHandler                 = 0x80044724;
// NPC slot handler per project_npc_handler_protocol.md. 2 spawn-sites (via func_80061268 caller wrapper, used by 6+ callers in funcs_13/36/37). Action 1 (spawn): two rand_recomp calls — first stamps random halfword at slot+0x10, second ORs random bits (0x1C00 mask, 3 bits) into the same halfword giving 8 random sub-states. Reads scale float f6 = msg+0x8, computes f0 = (rand_byte/const_at_0x8003B304 + const_at_0x8003B308) * f6 → stores at slot+0x20 AND slot+0x24 (current scale + max scale). Reads vec3 from MEM[msg+0x0] → slot+0x30/+0x34/+0x38 (position). Calls popNpcSlotFromTail to claim. The random-bit init + random scale + per-instance position pattern is characteristic of bursty particle/effect children spawned in groups — likely smoke puffs, sparks, or small debris with per-instance scale variation. Sibling wrapper `spawnNpcType0xAFromTemplate` (already named at 0x800612A4) takes a caller-supplied scale float; this handler is invoked from both that and `spawnRandomScaleEffectDefault`.
randomlyScaledEffectNpcHandler          = 0x80060690;
// 6+ callers (across funcs_13/36/37). Sister of spawnNpcType0xAFromTemplate (0x800612A4). Args: (a0=position vec3 ptr). Stores a0 at sp+0x10, fixed-constant float MEM[0x8003B3F8] at sp+0x18 (the default scale param), then calls spawnNpcOfType(handler=randomlyScaledEffectNpcHandler, descriptor=sp+0x10, type=1, subtype=0xA). The "default-scale" wrapper — for callers that don't need to tune the scale individually.
spawnRandomScaleEffectDefault           = 0x80061268;
// Sister of randomlyScaledEffectNpcHandler (0x80060690), identical dispatch shape per project_npc_handler_protocol.md. 2 spawn-sites via func_800612E0 caller wrapper. Action 1: same rand_recomp + OR 0x1C00 mask pattern as the sibling, but also stamps a per-pixel RGBA = (0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF) at slot+0x2C/+0x2D/+0x2E/+0x2F — initial color is white opaque, presumably modulated over the effect's lifetime. Stamps type id 0x300 at slot+0x2C (vs 0x100 in the sister). The "colored variant" of the random-scale particle handler — used for effects that need their tint to fade or pulse over time (e.g. spark trails, glow halos, alpha-faded smoke).
randomlyScaledColoredEffectNpcHandler   = 0x80060900;
// NPC slot handler per project_npc_handler_protocol.md. 1 spawn-site from cutsceneActorNpcHandler (i.e., spawned BY cutscene actors as a sub-NPC). Action 1 allocates rs_malloc(0xF14=3860 bytes) — the largest NPC context observed, much bigger than craft NPCs (484 bytes). Reads game-state bytes at MEM[0x80130B39] (scene-state) and MEM[0x80130B40] (cutscene-active flag, checks for value 0x13). Body uses heavy spline-walker math primitives (sqrt + normalize_vector + initVec4TripletFromArgs + composeRotationMatrixSlot0 + composeRotationMatrixSlot2) — same pattern as the spline-following NPC update functions per project_spline_based_architecture.md. The 3860-byte context + cinematic-actor spawn lineage + spline math => this is a heavy cinematic NPC that follows a pre-defined path through a cutscene (scripted craft flybys, scripted enemy movements).
cinematicSplineWalkerNpcHandler         = 0x8005BCC4;
// NPC slot handler per project_npc_handler_protocol.md. Action 1 (spawn): stores msg at slot+0x4, reads msg+0x10/+0x14/+0x18 (vec3 position) → slot's child struct +0x8/+0xC/+0x10, clears flags at child+0x48/+0x4C/+0x4D. Looks up parent NPC's mesh-part data via MEM[a2+0xA4] (mesh part ref), then halfword index at MEM[a2+0xA8]+0xA6 — uses that to index into the active-craft spatial grid at MEM[0x801375D8]+0x34 (the same grid documented in `updateActiveGridCellState`) to fetch an animation entry. Reads animation pose float from MEM[entry+0x10] to slot+0x10. The grid-driven animation lookup suggests this is an **animated sub-effect mounted to an active player/enemy craft** — e.g., a per-craft particle/effect that plays through the same animation system as the craft's mesh parts (engine flame, hit flash, etc.).
craftMountedAnimationNpcHandler         = 0x800ECC28;
// Sister of craftMountedAnimationNpcHandler in funcs_40.c. NPC slot handler per project_npc_handler_protocol.md but with extended action set (1/2/3/7 instead of standard 1/2/3/4). Action 1 (spawn): reads vec3 from msg+0x10/+0x14/+0x18 → stores at slot+0x4/+0x8/+0xC (position), zero-initializes velocity vec3 at slot+0x10/+0x14/+0x18 and another vec3 at slot+0x28/+0x2C/+0x30 (angular velocity / scale-derivative), zeroes slot+0x34/+0x38. Calls popNpcSlotFromTail. Action 2 (destroy): stamps 0xFFFF at slot+0x6 (mark inactive), then iterates entries comparing slot+0x30 vs slot+0x6C, and on each iteration calls **parseDatItemCommon** — the canonical DAT item parser. The use of parseDatItemCommon on cleanup is diagnostic: this NPC owns a DAT-item-defined entity (level-placed spawn point, trigger, breakable prop, etc.). 320-byte stack frame indicates substantial tick work in Action 3.

Then reads TWO source pointers (msg+0x0 and msg+0x4) representing two parent NPC positions, and writes the per-axis sum (parent1.x+parent2.x, parent1.y+parent2.y, parent1.z+parent2.z) into slot+0x0/+0x4/+0x8. Initializes a 6-float block at slot+0xC..+0x20 with constants (probably an identity-ish transform from the +0x14/+0x1C reads of 0.0/constant). Computes a scaled parameter (halfword at parent1+0x70 multiplied by two constants at 0x8003D7EC/0x8003D7F0) and writes to slot+0x34. Pattern of "two source positions + fixed mesh asset" is characteristic of bridging/connecting visuals between two NPCs (e.g., tow-cable segments, weapon links, animated tether visuals).
